### Pinned envelope investigation establishes timestamp compatibility defect, 14 November 2026

User requested the next source-investigation step. Pinned upstream commit `086ccc833bc9cf01024ad2dd6af7dbd421b6bf9a` wraps server notifications in `ServerNotificationEnvelope`, adding **`emittedAtMs`**. Its Rust definition flattens the notification and declares an optional signed 64-bit timestamp; the outgoing sender always supplies it and a source serialization test asserts the camelCase field. The frozen notification schema already declares this field at its root properties; the classifier extracts only method names from its oneOf variants and hard-codes a field set that overlooks the timestamp. Independent review DR-01 corrected the initial schema-omission explanation. A pure in-memory reproduction accepted a bare configWarning and rejected the same notification with emittedAtMs=1234. Ten saved source files match GitHub's returned Git blob hashes; this is source evidence, not a fresh binary build attestation.

This establishes a compatibility defect and strongly explains the one-extra-field rejection. The actual failed envelope's extra-field identity remains UNKNOWN because it was not retained. Startup configuration warnings are sent after initialization and can arrive during the thread/start wait. Producers include invalid configuration fallback, SQLite recovery, execution policy, disabled project configuration, core startup warnings and Linux sandbox prerequisites. Missing bubblewrap/user-namespace warnings are plausible, not proven; no install or sandbox-policy change is justified. The old v3 cause and actual warning text remain unknown.

The concrete next correction is to preserve v4, admit only the documented timestamp on recognized non-error notifications with exact integer/range validation, retain all other strict checks, and record bounded known warning categories even for accepted notifications. Do not globally ignore extra fields or suppress warnings. Cover malformed timestamp/types, misplaced timestamp, unknown extras/methods, errors, duplicate keys and warning redaction/no-match/report bounds offline, then freeze and independently review/QA before any launch request. This checkpoint proposes the correction only; it does not implement or authorize a runtime attempt.

## Later discussion and verification priorities

Resume with the user's chosen topic, keeping these areas visible when relevant:

- Review the architecture choice and boundaries before implementation. SDK session support alone does not prove that this runner meets the full requirements.
- Preserve the main AI Lead's design/interface responsibility alongside deterministic scheduling; validate how child-worker control fits current teamwork rules.
- Before implementation, turn container authentication, permitted mounts/network, trusted configuration, and controller credential separation into concrete tested setup. Do not mistake workspace-write permissions for complete host isolation.
- Keep the explicitly bounded single-host guarantee. A local database lock is not a distributed lock; moving hosts must stop/revoke the old publisher before granting new authority.
- Keep readiness/scope approval separate from current-code evidence. Existing approval should not be invalidated by status-only edits, but changed requirements and permissions need reconciliation.
- Resource limits are proposed defaults. Token thresholds, role profiles, and project check commands still need explicit configuration and validation; subscription usage does not imply exact dollar accounting.
- Preserve the finite-run behavior, retry history, human-review wait state, merge reconciliation, manual acceptance, and separately authorized deployment.
